People try to tell me it's just oversight, but... I'm not stupid. I can just tell they're doing it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing this tracking and the drivers reporting on you? Did this start suddenly, or has it been going on for a long time?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess it's been about two months. Give or take. It just... became obvious around then.\n\nDoctor: In these past two months, have you noticed any changes in your mood—like periods where you felt unusually energetic or depressed? Or have you experienced any strange sensory experiences, like hearing voices or seeing things others don't see?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my mood is fine. Just normal. And I don't... I don't see things that aren't there. It's just the computer and the radio stuff. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Besides the tracking and the radio comments from your coworkers, have you noticed anything else unusual happening—like people following you outside of work, or anything else that seems directed at you?\n\nPatient: No. Um... not really. Just the stuff at work. \n\nWhen I'm off the clock, I just go home and... it's fine. I haven't seen anyone following me or anything like that. It's just what I already told you.\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about why they might be tracking you and reporting on you? Do you have any sense of what their purpose or goal is in doing this?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I mean... I haven't really figured that part out. I just know they're doing it. I don't know why.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. That's when I really started noticing the computer tracking and the stuff on the radio. It just... became obvious around then.\n\nDoctor: In these past two months, have you noticed any changes in your mood—like periods where you felt unusually energetic or depressed? Or have you experienced any strange sensory experiences, like hearing voices or seeing things others don't see?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my mood is fine. Just normal. And I don't... I don't see things that aren't there. It's just the computer and the radio stuff. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Besides the tracking and the radio comments from your coworkers, have you noticed anything else unusual happening—like people following you outside of work, or anything else that seems directed at you?\n\nPatient: No. Um... not really. Just the stuff at work. \n\nWhen I'm off the clock, I just go home and... it's fine. I haven't seen anyone following me or anything like that. It's just what I already told you.\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about why they might be tracking you and reporting on you? Do you have any sense of what their purpose or goal is in doing this?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I mean... I haven't really figured that part out. I just know they're doing it. I don't know why.\n\nDoctor: When you think about why they're tracking you and making those comments—do you believe this is connected to something specific about you, or is it just routine workplace monitoring that happens to everyone?\n\nPatient: Um... well, people tell me it's just normal oversight. Like it happens to everyone. But... I don't think so. \n\nDispatch and compliance... they track my route changes on the computer system. And the little comments on the radio... I can just tell that certain drivers are reporting on me. It's not just routine. It's... me.\n\nDoctor: When these drivers are reporting on you and dispatch is tracking your routes—do you believe they're doing this because they have something against you personally, or do you think there might be some other reason they're singling you out?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know if it's personal. I just... \n\nLike I said, I just know dispatch and compliance are tracking my route changes on the computer. And I hear those little comments on the radio. People keep telling me it's just normal oversight, but... I know those drivers are reporting on me. I don't really know what their reason is. I just know they're doing it.\n\nDoctor: If I told you that dispatch tracks everyone's routes as part of standard fleet management, and that radio comments are just normal workplace communication—would that explanation make sense to you, or do you still feel certain they're specifically targeting you?\n\nPatient: I mean... you could say that. But... I don't know. \n\nI just know what I see. The computer system... it's tracking my route changes. And the radio... those comments. I know people say it's just normal oversight, but... I can just tell. I know certain drivers are reporting on me. It's not just standard.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

Through the computer system. And making these little comments on the radio. \n\nPeople keep saying it's just normal oversight, but... I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nDoctor: When you think about the drivers reporting on you and the tracking through the computer system—do you have any doubt that this is actually happening, or are you completely certain it's real?\n\nPatient: I mean... I'm certain. I wouldn't have brought it up if it wasn't happening. \n\nLike I just said, I know what I'm seeing on the system. And I hear what they say on the radio. People try to tell me it's just oversight, but... I'm not stupid. I can just tell they're doing it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing this tracking and the drivers reporting on you? Did this start suddenly, or has it been going on for a long time?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess it's been about two months. Give or take. That's when I really started noticing the computer tracking and the stuff on the radio. It just... became obvious around then.\n\nDoctor: In these past two months, have you noticed any changes in your mood—like periods where you felt unusually energetic or depressed? Or have you experienced any strange sensory experiences, like hearing voices or seeing things others don't see?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my mood is fine. Just normal. And I don't... I don't see things that aren't there. It's just the computer and the radio stuff. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Besides the tracking and the radio comments from your coworkers, have you noticed anything else unusual happening—like people following you outside of work, or anything else that seems directed at you?\n\nPatient: No. Um... not really. Just the stuff at work. \n\nWhen I'm off the clock, I just go home and... it's fine. I haven't seen anyone following me or anything like that. It's just what I already told you.\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about why they might be tracking you and reporting on you? Do you have any sense of what their purpose or goal is in doing this?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I mean... I haven't really figured that part out. I just know they're doing it. I don't know why.\n\nDoctor: When you think about why they're tracking you and making those comments—do you believe this is connected to something specific about you, or is it just routine workplace monitoring that happens to everyone?\n\nPatient: Um... well, people tell me it's just normal oversight. Like it happens to everyone. But... I don't think so. \n\nDispatch and compliance... they track my route changes on the computer system. And the little comments on the radio... I can just tell that certain drivers are reporting on me. It's not just routine. It's... me.\n\nDoctor: When these drivers are reporting on you and dispatch is tracking your routes—do you believe they're doing this because they have something against you personally, or do you think there might be some other reason they're singling you out?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know if it's personal. I just... \n\nLike I said, I just know dispatch and compliance are tracking my route changes on the computer. And I hear those little comments on the radio. People keep telling me it's just normal oversight, but... I know those drivers are reporting on me. I don't really know what their reason is. I just know they're doing it.\n\nDoctor: If I told you that dispatch tracks everyone's routes as part of standard fleet management, and that radio comments are just normal workplace communication—would that explanation make sense to you, or do you still feel certain they're specifically targeting you?\n\nPatient: I mean... you could say that. But... I don't know. \n\nI just know what I see. The computer system... it's tracking my route changes. And the radio... those comments. I know people say it's just normal oversight, but... I can just tell. I know certain drivers are reporting on me. It's not just standard.\n\nDoctor: If you were to talk to your supervisor or someone in management, and they showed you the actual computer logs and explained exactly how the tracking system works for all drivers—would that change your mind about what's happening, or would you still believe they're specifically targeting you?\n\nPatient: I... probably not. They could show me whatever they want, but... it wouldn't change what I know. \n\nThey can keep saying it's just normal oversight. But... the computer system is still tracking my route changes. And I still hear the little comments on the radio. I can just tell those drivers are reporting on me. So... no. Looking at their logs wouldn't really change anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about whether all of this—the tracking, the radio comments, the drivers reporting on you—might be connected to each other as part of one larger plan, or do these things feel separate to you?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know about a plan. I don't really want to guess about that. \n\nLike I've been saying... it's all just happening together. Dispatch and compliance with the computer tracking, the comments on the radio... and the drivers reporting on me. Everyone keeps telling me it's normal oversight, but... I know it's not. \n\nI don't know if they're all secretly working together or something. I just know they're all doing it.\n\nDoctor: When you think about dispatch tracking your routes, the radio comments, and the drivers reporting on you—do these feel like separate things happening, or do you see them as all connected together as part of one coordinated effort against you?\n\nPatient: I... I think I just answered that. I don't really want to guess if it's coordinated or... whatever. \n\nI just know what they're doing. Dispatch and compliance are tracking my route changes through the computer system. There are those little comments on the radio. And even with everyone telling me it's just normal oversight... I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me. \n\nI don't know how else to explain it. They're just... all doing it. That's all I know.\n\nDoctor: When you think about all of this—the tracking, the radio comments, and the drivers reporting on you—do these things feel like they fit together into a clear system or pattern, or do they feel more like separate incidents that just happen to be occurring?\n\nPatient: I... we just went over this. I don't know why you keep asking me the same thing. \n\nI don't know about a clear system or a pattern. I'm just telling you what's happening. Dispatch and compliance track my route changes on the computer. I hear the little comments on the radio. And even when people tell me it's just normal oversight... I can tell those drivers are reporting on me. \n\nThat's it. That's all I know. I don't really have anything else to say about how they fit together.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I don't know why.\n\nDoctor: When you think about why they're tracking you and making those comments—do you believe this is connected to something specific about you, or is it just routine workplace monitoring that happens to everyone?\n\nPatient: Um... well, people tell me it's just normal oversight. I just know they're doing it.\n\nDoctor: If I told you that dispatch tracks everyone's routes as part of standard fleet management, and that radio comments are just normal workplace communication—would that explanation make sense to you, or do you still feel certain they're specifically targeting you?\n\nPatient: I mean... you could say that. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I haven't done anything wrong. I don't... I don't know what's in their heads or what they think. 

I just drive my routes. I don't know why it's me they're doing this to. I really can't give you an answer for this... I just don't know.
